A cell that applies is drawn with `Visualize.Chart.render/2` as a responsive root titled with the cell's title, its id `<id>-<cell>` with `<id>` `preview/3`'s `id:` option (default `vis-builder-board`), so two cells drawing one design never share a root's ids or its dark-mode rule (#712, D-188); one that does not lists its faults in its card, as the preview does (§18.4). **A cell whose binding has the wrong type is not realized** (D-153): the binding's type faults (§22.8) for that cell are drawn as the fault panel of §18.4 with their lines beneath it, and so is a cell whose application reports a `{:type_mismatch, …}`; the panel takes the cell's root id `<id>-<cell>`, standing where its chart would ([15-boards](15-boards.md) §8.4, #722).

**The preview is the builder's model: sample data, no process.** A board's slots are the cells' ([15-boards](15-boards.md) §4.1), and each cell's pool binds them **through the binding** (§22.8): `Visualize.Board.Binding.pools/2` of the binding and the sample rows of each bindable feed. A catalog feed's rows are the host's `sources` entry of the feed's name when there is one, else rows made from its schema (`Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.sample/2`) ending at the instant the host gave the catalog — made once then, beside the catalog's types (§19.10, D-189, D-191) — carried as a `Visualize.Data.Delivery` naming the schema's version, and each slot is described from the catalog through its feed, so a slot that states a contract is checked as the runtime checks it; with no catalog a feed is a pool entry, the host's `sources` and the builder's signals (§19.10). A slot the binding leaves unbound has no source, and its cell lists `{:unbound, :source, slot}` as application words it. A live preview through `Visualize.Board.Server` and the board's LiveView component was not taken: it needs feeds, which the builder does not hold and a host need not give it, and a process per open board the builder would have to start, supervise and stop. The board the builder saves is the one the runtime runs, so what the preview applies is what the runtime applies.

**A cell's preview is drawn again only when one of its inputs moved** (#723, D-191). A preview is a function of the cell, the board's shared state (everything but its cells), what the store says of the cell's component, the cell's pool, the theme, the catalog with the feeds the cell's slots are bound to, and the type faults that refuse it; the builder keeps each cell's preview in its assigns under those inputs, as a plain map — no process, no table outside the socket — refreshed after every event and every `update/2`, and a pass draws only the cells whose inputs differ from the ones kept. A pass given inputs other than the kept ones draws those cells itself, so a preview is never stale; an event that touches one cell draws that cell alone.

### 22.7 Edit component

*Edit component* (`board_edit_component`) opens the selected cell's composite as a chart of the workspace, as opening the entry from the library does (§19.9), so *Save to library* writes the component back and every cell that uses it previews the change. The chart remembers the board it came from: its chart bar carries ***Back to board*** (`board_return`), which selects the board's chart again. **Returning closes the component's chart when it holds no unsaved change** — its structure is the one it was opened with, or the one the store now holds — and leaves it open, with a notice, when it does, so nothing a person did is dropped. A component the store no longer holds is not opened, and the notice says so. A board's chart closed in the meantime leaves *Back to board* with nothing to return to, and the notice says that too.

### 22.8 Binding the slots: the connector catalog

**The builder learns a host's feeds from a catalog.** `catalog` (§18.2) maps each feed the host can bind to its schema, which is what the feed's `describe` answers (spec/08 §10.3) — a host makes it from its feeds with `Visualize.Feed.describe/1`, or from wherever it keeps their descriptions. The catalog's keys are the **bindable feeds**. With no catalog, the bindable feeds are the sample pool's names, the host's `sources` and the builder's signals, so a board is bound and previewed with nothing from the host but samples.

**The slots are the board's required sources** ([15-boards](15-boards.md) §4.1), each cell's read through `Visualize.Board.required_sources/3` of a board holding that cell alone, so a cell whose component does not resolve leaves the others' slots listed. In the inspector the selected cell's section has a *sources* part (`<form class="vis-builder-board">`, `board_bind`): one select per slot, `slot.<name>`, of *unbound* and every bindable feed, the feed the binding names chosen. A feed from the page is matched against the bindable feeds by its printed form and never made a term of its own. Under each slot are its faults.

**The faults are the board's binding check.** `Visualize.Board.Binding.check/3` of the binding, the required sources and the catalog — the catalog is the schemas map that check takes; with no catalog it is `%{}`, and a sample has no schema — so the builder shows, before any data flows, what the runtime would report ([15-boards](15-boards.md) §4.3): every unbound slot (`{:unbound, :binding, slot}`), every attribute a slot's contract requires that the feed's schema does not give (`{:missing_attribute, slot, field, key}`), every declared type the schema contradicts (`{:schema_type, field, declared, described}`), every field the cell's component reads through a scale whose type the scale cannot take (`{:type_mismatch, slot, field, expected, given}`, D-153), a contract over a feed with no schema (`{:undescribed, feed}`), and a binding to a slot no cell has (`:not_required`), each in `Visualize.Board.format_error/1`'s words. Because the preview draws rows, `Visualize.Board.Binding.check_types/3` also checks the binding against the types each bindable feed's preview rows show (`Visualize.Data.Table.column_types/1`), catalog or not. So a sample with no schema is checked, and so are a catalog feed's sample rows, whose `:time` field is instants. The slot's own faults stand under its select, a type fault under the slot it names, and the *validity* section lists them all beside the board's.

**The binding is the host's, and leaves as a message.** The binding is never in the board: *Save to library* stores the board alone, and a bundle carries none (D-147). It lives on the board's chart while a person works, and every change of it is sent to the host as `{on_bind, id, board_id, binding}` — `id` the component's, `board_id` the board entry's id, `nil` for a board not yet saved, and `binding` the whole `%Visualize.Board.Binding{}` — from the component's own callback, so the host's `handle_info/2` decides where a binding is kept, as it decides what a saved design means (§18.2). A host gives one back through `board_bindings` when the board is opened again.

**Where a binding starts.** Opening a board takes the host's binding for its id from `board_bindings`; a board the host holds none for, and every cell added, starts with each of its unbound slots **bound by name** to the bindable feed of the slot's own name, when there is one — the rule the preview of one design binds by (§7.3). A slot with no such feed starts unbound and says so. Removing a cell removes its slots' bindings, and the change is sent.

### 22.9 Virtual sources in the board mode

**Implemented** (#643, work item #753, D-194). A board's virtual sources (spec/08 §10.5, [15-boards](15-boards.md) §14) run in the board mode **as the board runs them, over the preview's data**: the bindable feeds' sample rows (§22.6), each a delivery under its catalog schema, are pushed to a `Visualize.VirtualSource.Engine` of the board's sources and the host's `virtual_sources` modules, and one pulse runs at once (`debounce: 0`), so every source has an answer, or a fault, from the rows the cells preview. No process is started: the engine is a value, and its calls are the sandbox's bounded calls (spec/08 §10.5.4).

- **The cells preview over them.** A slot bound to a virtual source previews over its answer; a slot bound to a feed previews over the feed's rows with the joined fields of every per-identity source over it ([15-boards](15-boards.md) §14.7), described in its schema. So a cell that styles by `{:field, :"health.state"}` or chooses by it previews as the board draws it, and the binding check (§22.8) reads each feed's schema with its joined fields — a fact of a joined field (`fact(:hosts, :"health.state", :values)`) is no fault — where a cell reads the board's own sources.
- **The sources section.** The inspector's *virtual sources* section (`Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.panel/1`, after *variables*) lists each source by its dotted path: its state (`loading`, `live`, or its fault's kind), a **text box holding its function** — `<textarea name="elixir">` for a stored function; the host's captured source, read-only, for a `defsource` (spec/08 §10.5.8) — its faults inline, each `line N: …` in `Visualize.VirtualSource.Sandbox.format_error/1`'s words, and its **preview rows**: the first five rows of its answer, a table of its output's fields.
- **Editing the text** (`board_source`, with the source's dotted path and the text). The text is checked as the board would check it — `Visualize.VirtualSource.new/1`, then `validate/2` given its inputs' schemas where they are known, so a field a function reads that no input has is named. A text that holds is **written** into the board (its `virtual_sources`, or the cell's), the board validated alone as every write is (§22.2), and the sources run again over the same rows, so the preview rows and every cell that reads the source change as it is typed. A text the checker refuses is **kept as a draft** beside its faults — the board keeps the last function that held, and the preview its rows — so a half-typed function is never lost and never written while the board stays open; opening a board shows its stored text.
- **Faults stay where they are.** A source whose function raises, times out, passes its heap limit or answers rows outside its schema on the preview's rows shows its fault's kind and detail (spec/08 §10.5.4) and keeps its last answer; nothing else in the mode is held back by it. A board whose sources cannot be defined — an input no feed or source names, a cycle — lists those faults in the section, and its cells preview over the feeds alone.
- **Kept by its inputs.** The sources' answers are a function of the board's sources, the binding, the bindable feeds' rows and schemas and the host's modules; a pass keeps them under those (§22.5, D-191) and runs the engine only when one moved.

### 22.10 Functions

| Function | Contract |
|---|---|
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.panel/1` | the inspector of the board mode (§22.4) |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.BoardGrid.grid/1` | the grid of the board mode (§22.3): every cell placed, its handles and its preview, or the reflow below the breakpoint |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.place/3` | `place(board, cell, rect)`: the board with the cell at the rectangle, `{:ok, board}`, or `{:error, errors}` — the board's own faults of the placement, `{:off_grid, 12}` and `{:overlap, other}` among them (§22.3) |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.free/3` | `free(board, w, h)`: the first `{x, y}` in reading order where a `w`-by-`h` rectangle overlaps no cell (§22.3) |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.declared/2` | `declared(ref, fetch)`: the variables a component declares, from its flattened design, sorted (§22.4) |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.sample/2` | `sample(schema, now)`: sixty rows made from a schema for a preview (§22.6): a `:time` field a second apart and ending at `now`, a `:number` window field (`window: true`) its row's place, `0` to `59`, as a tick counts, any other `:number` field a slow sine, a `:category` field its `order` in turn (else `"a"`, `"b"`, `"c"`), a `:text` field its name |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.sources/1` | `sources(mode)`: the board mode's view of its virtual sources (§22.9) — each one's path, state, function text, faults and preview rows |
| `Visualize.Chart.Builder.Boards.preview/3` | `preview(board, fetch, opts)`: each cell realized alone (§22.6), `%{cell => {:ok, svg} \| {:error, lines}}`; `pools:` by cell, `theme:`, and `describe:` as `Visualize.Board.realize/4` takes it |
